Schematic representation of the extended domain
\Omega_{\beta}^{+}
satisfying (
100
), depicted here in the vicinity of
z_{i}
, a critical point close to the boundary, but outside the domain
\Omega_{\beta}
.

4.6
Conclusion of the proof of Theorem
4

We conclude this section by giving the proof of the harmonic approximation in the case of a temperature-dependent boundary. We show that the first-order
\beta\to\infty
asymptotics of any given eigenvalue at the bottom of the spectrum of
H_{\beta}
are given by the corresponding eigenvalue of the global harmonic approximation
H_{\beta,\alpha}^{\mathrm{H}}
defined in (
76
).
The proof extends the results of
[
18
,
59
]
to the case of moving Dirichlet boundary conditions, allowing the computation of first-order spectral asymptotics for eigenstates localized (in the Witten representation) around critical points which are close to the boundary.
To show (
37
), we study the eigenvalues of the Witten Laplacian (
19
), since these are equal to those of
-\mathcal{L}_{\beta}
up to a factor of
\beta
.
The (standard) strategy we follow is to construct approximate eigenvectors for
H_{\beta}
, which (roughly speaking) consist of eigenvectors of each of the local oscillators
H_{\beta,\alpha^{(i)}}^{(i)}
, localized around the critical points
z_{i}
by appropriate cutoff functions.
The main technical novelty compared to previous works, besides the construction of critical harmonic models performed in Section
4.2
, is the technique used to ensure that the quasimodes belong to the form domain of the Dirichlet Witten Laplacian on
\Omega_{\beta}
, relying namely on Proposition
12
, rather than on the fact that the domain is locally diffeomorphic to a half-space, as in
[
27
]
and
[
46
]
.

Once we have constructed valid quasimodes, we obtain coarse estimates similar to those of
[
18
,
59
]
, at the level of the quadratic form and Courant–Fischer variational principles, allowing us to compute the limit spectrum of
\mathcal{L}_{\beta}
as
\beta\to\infty
, explicitly in terms of the spectra of the Dirichlet oscillators of Section
4.2
.
The constant
\beta_{0}>0
will be increased a finite number of times in the following proof, without changing notation.

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Without loss of generality, we assume in this proof that
\delta(\beta)<\beta^{s-\frac{1}{2}}
in (
H2
) for some
0<s<\frac{1}{6}
, so that the assumptions of Lemma
11
are satisfied.

4.6.1
Step 1: Upper bound on
\lambda_{k,\beta}

The proof of the upper bound proceeds in two steps. First, we choose an appropriate realization of the harmonic approximation (
76
) so that the associated quasimodes are in the form domain
H_{0}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta})
. The second step is essentially identical to the analysis performed in
[
18
, Theorem 11.1]
, given the localization estimates of Lemma
10
. We include it for the sake of completeness.

Step 1a: Perturbation of the local oscillators.

We fix
k\geq 1
. We construct families of quasimodes
\{\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{k}\}
for
H_{\beta}
associated with the first
k
harmonic eigenvalues
\beta\lambda_{1,\alpha}^{\mathrm{H}},\dots,\beta\lambda_{k,\alpha}^{\mathrm{H}}
.
Recall Table
1
for notation used in the definition of the harmonic approximation.
The functions
\varphi_{j}
are of the general form (
84
). We must however be careful with the choice of realization for the formal operator
H_{\beta}^{(i_{j})}
defined in (
46
), to ensure that the quasimodes are in the form domain of
H_{\beta}
.
In fact we need, for each
0\leq i<N
, to distinguish between two cases:

a)

If
z_{i}
is far from the boundary, then by Assumption (
H2
),
\chi_{\beta}^{(i)}
is supported inside
\Omega_{\beta}
, and thus the associated quasimodes are indeed in
H_{0}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta})
(and even in the domain of
\mathcal{L}_{\beta}
).

b)

If
z_{i}
is close to the boundary, then by the third condition in Assumption (
H2
),
\mathcal{O}_{i}^{-}(\beta)\subseteq B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))\cap\Omega_{\beta}
for
\beta
large enough. In this case, the construction presented below yields again a quasimode in
H_{0}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta})
.

Let
0\leq i<N
, and
h>0
. Then, Assumption (
H2
) implies that there exists
\beta_{0}>0
such that, for all
\beta>\beta_{0}
, we have the inclusion:

B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))\cap\left[z_{i}+E^{(i)}\left(\frac{\alpha^{(i)}-h}{\sqrt{\beta}}\right)\right]\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{i}^{-}(\beta)\subseteq\Omega_{\beta}.

Defining the vector

\alpha^{h,-}=(\alpha^{(i)}-h\mathbbm{1}_{\alpha^{(i)}<+\infty})_{0\leq i<N},

we consider the perturbed harmonic approximation corresponding to the operator
H_{\beta,\alpha^{h,-}}^{\mathrm{H}}
, as defined in (
76
).
By construction, for all
\beta>\beta_{0}
, eigenmodes
\psi_{\beta,n_{j},\alpha^{h,-}_{i_{j}}}^{(i_{j})}
of
H_{\beta,\alpha^{h,-}_{i_{j}}}^{(i_{j})}
are supported in

z_{i_{j}}+E^{(i_{j})}\left(\frac{\alpha^{(i_{j})}-h}{\sqrt{\beta}}\right)
,
as noted in (
74
), and it follows that the associated quasimode
\widetilde{\psi}_{\beta,n_{j},\alpha^{h,-}_{i_{j}}}^{(i_{j})}
, as defined by (
84
), belongs to
H_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{i_{j}}^{-}(\beta))\subset H_{0}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta})
.

Step 1b: Energy upper bound.

The aim of this step is to show the upper bound for all
k\geq 1
:

\underset{\beta\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\,\lambda_{k,\beta}\leq\lambda_{k,\alpha}^{\mathrm{H}}.

(106)

For a fixed
k\geq 1
, we consider the quasimodes

\varphi_{j}=\widetilde{\psi}_{\beta,n_{j},\alpha^{h,-}_{i_{j}}}^{(i_{j})}\qquad\text{for }1\leq j\leq k.

Note that
(\varphi_{j})_{1\leq j\leq k}
span a
k
-dimensional subspace of
H_{0}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta})
, since the Gram matrix

\left(\left\langle\varphi_{j},\varphi_{j^{\prime}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta})}\right)_{1\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq k}

is quasi-unitary, i.e. can be written in the form
I+\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(1)
as
\beta\to\infty
. Indeed, since the
\varphi_{j}
are normalized in
L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta})
, it suffices to check that the off-diagonal entries of the Gram matrix vanish in this limit.
Fixing
1\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq k
, this is clear for
i_{j}\neq i_{j^{\prime}}
, since the corresponding test functions
\chi_{\beta}^{(i_{j})}
,
\chi_{\beta}^{(i_{j^{\prime}})}
have disjoint supports. If
i_{j}=i_{j^{\prime}}
, the statement is an immediate consequence of the quasi-orthogonality estimate (
91
).

By the Min-Max Courant–Fischer principle, it suffices to show that

\forall\,u\in\mathrm{Span}\{\varphi_{j}\}_{\,1\leq j\leq k},\qquad Q_{\beta}(u)\leq(\beta\lambda_{k,\alpha^{h,-}}^{\mathrm{H}}+\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\beta))\|u\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta})}^{2},

(107)

to conclude

\underset{\beta\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\,\lambda_{k,\beta}\leq\lambda_{k,\alpha^{h,-}}^{\mathrm{H}}.

Since the map
h\mapsto\lambda_{k,\alpha^{h,-}}^{\mathrm{H}}
is continuous and
{\alpha^{h,-}\xrightarrow{h\to 0}\alpha}
,
and therefore
\lambda_{k,\alpha^{h,-}}^{\mathrm{H}}\xrightarrow{h\to 0}\lambda_{k,\alpha}^{\mathrm{H}}
, the latter inequality implies (
106
).

Let us prove (
107
). Unless otherwise specified, in Step 1, norms and inner products are on
L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta})
.
For a fixed
1\leq j\leq k
, first consider
u=\chi_{\beta}^{(i_{j})}\psi_{\beta,n_{j},\alpha^{h,-}}^{(i_{j})}\in\mathrm{Span}\{\varphi_{j}\}
. Recall that
H_{\beta,\alpha_{i_{j}}^{h,-}}^{(i_{j})}\psi_{\beta,n_{j},\alpha^{h,-}}^{(i_{j})}=\beta\lambda_{n_{j},\alpha^{h,-}}^{(i_{j})}\psi_{\beta,n_{j},\alpha^{h,-}}^{(i_{j})}
. For convenience, we drop the indices and superscripts in the following computation, so that
{u=\chi\psi}
, with
H\psi=\beta\lambda\psi
.
By our choice of
(\varphi_{j})_{1\leq j\leq k}
, we have
{\lambda\in\left\{\lambda_{j,\alpha^{h,-}}^{\mathrm{H}}\right\}_{1\leq j\leq k}}
, and so
\lambda\leq\lambda_{k,\alpha^{h,-}}^{\mathrm{H}}
.

Q_{\beta}(u)=\left\langle H_{\beta}u,u\right\rangle=\left\langle Hu,u\right\rangle+\left\langle(H_{\beta}-H)u,u\right\rangle,

and since
\left|\left\langle(H_{\beta}-H)u,u\right\rangle\right|\leq C_{j,\alpha,h}\beta^{3s+\frac{1}{2}}\|u\|^{2}=\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\beta)\|u\|^{2}
for some constant
C_{j,\alpha,h}>0
by Lemma
11
, the only remaining task is to estimate the first term.
Expanding the quadratic form, we get:

\displaystyle\left\langle H\chi\psi,\chi\psi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle H\psi,\psi\right\rangle-2\left\langle H\psi,(1-\chi)\psi\right\rangle+\left\langle H(1-\chi)\psi,(1-\chi)\psi\right\rangle

(108)

\displaystyle=\beta\lambda\|\psi\|^{2}-2\left\langle H\psi,(1-\chi)\psi\right\rangle+\left\langle H(1-\chi)\psi,(1-\chi)\psi\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\beta\lambda\|u\|^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\beta\|(1-\chi)\psi\|)+\left\langle H(1-\chi)\psi,(1-\chi)\psi\right\rangle,

where we used a Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the estimate (see (
90
))

\|\psi\|^{2}=\|u\|^{2}+2\left\langle u,(1-\chi)\psi\right\rangle+\|(1-\chi)\psi\|^{2}=\|u\|^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\|(1-\chi)\psi\|).

We next use the localization estimates given in Lemma
10
to control the two rightmost terms in the last line of (
108
). Here, we make crucial use of the hypothesis (
H3
),
which gives the superpolynomial decay of the bound (
90
), implying that
\beta\|(1-\chi)\psi\|=\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\beta)
, and similarly
\left\langle H(1-\chi)\psi,(1-\chi)\psi\right\rangle=\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\beta)
, using (
92
).
Finally, (
91
) implies that
\|u\|^{2}=1+\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(1)
, so that we may write
Q_{\beta}(u)\leq\|u\|^{2}(\beta\lambda+\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\beta))
.
Since
\lambda\leq\lambda_{k,\alpha^{h,-}}^{\mathrm{H}}
, this implies the upper bound (
107
) for this particular choice of
u
, and thus for any
u\in\mathrm{Span}\{\varphi_{j}\}
, as we now show.

For
u=\sum_{j=1}^{k}g_{j}\varphi_{j}\in\mathrm{Span}\{\varphi_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq k}
, in view of the previous estimate, it is enough to show that the cross terms

g_{j}g_{j^{\prime}}\left\langle H_{\beta,\alpha_{i_{j}}^{h,-}}^{(i_{j})}\varphi_{j},\varphi_{j^{\prime}}\right\rangle

are small whenever
i_{j}=i_{j^{\prime}}
with
j\neq j^{\prime}
, since the terms for which
i_{j}\neq i_{j^{\prime}}
vanish for
\beta
large enough as the corresponding cutoff functions have disjoint supports.
To check that the non-zero terms decay superpolynomially in
\beta
, we denote, for convenience
H_{\beta,\alpha_{i_{j}}^{h,-}}^{(i_{j})}=H
,
\chi=\chi_{\beta}^{(i_{j})}
, and
\psi=\psi^{(i_{j})}_{\beta,n_{j},\alpha^{h,-}}
,
\psi^{\prime}=\psi^{(i_{j^{\prime}})}_{\beta,n_{j^{\prime}},\alpha^{h,-}}
, so that
\varphi_{j}=\chi\psi/Z
,
\varphi_{j^{\prime}}=\chi\psi^{\prime}/Z^{\prime}
, where
Z,Z^{\prime}
are normalizing constants ensuring that the quasimodes have unit
L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta})
-norm. With this notation,

\displaystyle\left\langle H\chi\psi,\chi\psi^{\prime}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle H\psi,\psi^{\prime}\right\rangle-\left\langle H\psi,(1-\chi)\psi^{\prime}\right\rangle-\left\langle H(1-\chi)\psi,\psi^{\prime}\right\rangle+\left\langle H(1-\chi)\psi,(1-\chi)\psi^{\prime}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle H\psi,\psi^{\prime}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda\left\langle\psi,(1-\chi)\psi^{\prime}\right\rangle-\beta\lambda^{\prime}\left\langle(1-\chi)\psi,\psi^{\prime}\right\rangle+\left\langle H(1-\chi)\psi,(1-\chi)\psi^{\prime}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=0+\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\beta)\|\psi\|\|\psi^{\prime}\|,

where we used again Cauchy–Schwarz inequalities and the superpolynomial decay of the estimates (
90
) and (
92
) under (
H3
), as well as the orthogonality relation
\left\langle\psi,\psi^{\prime}\right\rangle=0
.
Since
Z,Z^{\prime}=1+\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(1)
by (
91
), it follows that (
107
) holds, which concludes the proof of (
106
).

4.6.2
Step 2: Lower bound on
\lambda_{k,\beta}

We show the lower bound in (
37
), namely that for all
k\geq 1
,

\underset{\beta\to\infty}{\underline{\lim}}\,\lambda_{k,\beta}\geq\lambda_{k,\alpha}^{\mathrm{H}}.

(109)

As in the proof of the upper bound, we proceed in two steps.
The first step is to construct an appropriate extension of the domain
\Omega_{\beta}
around critical points which are close to the boundary, relying on Proposition
12
.
The second step is similar to what is done in related proofs of
[
59
,
18
]
in the boundaryless case, and we include it for completeness.

Step 2a: Perturbation of the domain.

Our analysis requires us to consider, in addition to perturbed harmonic eigenvalues, a perturbed domain
\Omega_{\beta}^{h}
which contains
\Omega_{\beta}
, parametrized by some small
h>0
.
We construct
\Omega_{\beta}^{h}
to be smooth and bounded, so that the Dirichlet realization of
H_{\beta}
in
\Omega_{\beta}^{h}
is self-adjoint on
L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})
, with compact resolvent and form domain
H_{0}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})
.
This domain is constructed by a direct application of Proposition
12
with
\rho(\beta)=\frac{h}{\sqrt{\beta}}
, that is,

\Omega_{\beta}^{h}=\Omega_{\beta,h/\sqrt{\beta}}^{+}.

Crucially, it satisfies the inclusion (
100
), which corresponds to the requirement that, locally around each
z_{i}
close to the boundary,
\partial\Omega_{\beta}^{h}
coincides precisely with a hyperplane located at a distance
\beta^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\alpha^{(i)}+h)
away from the saddle in the direction
v^{(i)}_{1}
, and normal to the same vector.
In the remainder of the proof, norms and inner products are by default on
L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})
.
We denote by
\lambda^{h}_{k,\beta}:=\lambda_{k,\beta}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})
its
k
-th eigenvalue, which is positive with finite multiplicity, and by
H_{\beta}^{h}
the Dirichlet realization of
H_{\beta}
on
\Omega_{\beta}^{h}
, with
Q_{\beta}^{h}
its associated quadratic form.
By the comparison principle given in Proposition
17
, it holds that
\lambda_{k,\beta}(\Omega_{\beta})\geq\lambda_{k,\beta}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})
, and so it is sufficient to prove (
109
) with
\lambda_{k,\beta}=\lambda_{k,\beta}^{h}
.

Step 2b: Energy lower bound.

From now on,
\alpha^{h,+}
denotes the vector
(\alpha^{(i)}+h\mathbbm{1}_{\alpha^{(i)}<+\infty})_{0\leq i<N}
and
\varphi_{j}
, for
1\leq j\leq k-1
, are
k-1
harmonic quasimodes, associated with the Dirichlet harmonic approximation
H^{\mathrm{H}}_{\beta,\alpha^{h,+}}
.
However, due to the construction performed in the previous step, one must slightly adjust the definition (
84
), and more precisely the support of the cutoff function.
For this, we set

\varphi_{j}=\frac{\eta_{\beta}^{(i_{j})}\psi_{\beta,n_{j},\alpha^{h,+}_{i_{j}}}^{(i_{j})}}{\|\eta_{\beta}^{(i_{j})}\psi_{\beta,n_{j},\alpha^{h,+}_{i_{j}}}^{(i_{j})}\|},\qquad\forall\,1\leq j\leq k-1,

where
\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}(x-z_{i})=\chi\left(2|x-z_{i}|/\delta(\beta)\right)
.
By construction, each
\varphi_{j}
is supported in
B(z_{i_{j}},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta))
, and thus belongs to
H_{0}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})
, by the inclusion (
100
) and the fact that
\psi_{\beta,n_{j},\alpha^{h,+}_{i_{j}}}^{(i_{j})}
has support in
z_{i_{j}}+E^{(i_{j})}\left(\frac{\alpha^{(i_{j})}+h}{\sqrt{\beta}}\right)
whenever
\alpha^{(i_{j})}<+\infty
.
We note that this choice of quasimodes amounts to rescaling
\delta(\beta)
by a factor
\frac{1}{2}
, and consequently has no impact on the superpolynomial decay of the estimates of Lemma
10
, nor on the conclusion of Lemma
11
, which we will use freely in the remainder of the proof, upon replacing
\chi_{\beta}^{(i)}
by
\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}
,
\Omega_{\beta}
by
\Omega_{\beta}^{h}
and
H_{\beta}
by
H_{\beta}^{h}
.
As in Step 1b, for
\beta
large enough, the
\varphi_{j}
span a
(k-1)
-dimensional subspace of
L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})
.

This time, we rely on the Courant–Fischer principle in its Max-Min form: it suffices to show that, for any
u\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})\cap\mathrm{Span}\{\varphi_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq k-1}^{\perp}
, the following inequality holds:

Q^{h}_{\beta}(u)\geq(\beta\lambda_{k,\alpha^{h,+}}^{\mathrm{H}}+\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\beta))\|u\|^{2}.

This will indeed imply:

\underset{\beta\to\infty}{\underline{\lim}}\,\lambda_{k,\beta}\geq\underset{\beta\to\infty}{\underline{\lim}}\,\lambda^{h}_{k,\beta}\geq\lambda_{k,\alpha^{h,+}}^{\mathrm{H}},

and the desired lower bound (
109
) follows by continuity of the harmonic eigenvalues with respect to the boundary position
\alpha^{h,+}
, taking the limit
h\to 0
.
Hence, let
u\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})
be orthogonal to
\varphi_{j}
for every
1\leq j\leq k-1
. The IMS localization formula (see for instance
[
18
, Chapter 3]
) gives:

Q^{h}_{\beta}(u)=\sum_{i=0}^{N}\left[Q^{h}_{\beta}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u\right)-\left\|u\nabla\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\right],

(110)

with
\eta_{\beta}^{(N)}=\sqrt{\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{\beta}^{h}}-\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\eta_{\beta}^{(i)2}}.

We first estimate, for
0\leq i<N
, the terms

Q_{\beta}^{h}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u\right)-\left\langle H_{\beta,\alpha^{h,+}_{i}}^{(i)}\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u,\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u\right\rangle=\left\langle(H_{\beta}^{h}-H_{\beta,\alpha_{i}^{h,+}}^{(i)})\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u,\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u\right\rangle=\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\beta)\|u\|^{2},

(111)

using Lemma
11
.
On the other hand, the assumption that
u
is
L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})
-orthogonal to the
\varphi_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq k-1
implies, for all
0\leq i<N
and all
1\leq n\leq\mathfrak{N}_{i}(k-1)
, that
\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u
is
L^{2}\left[z_{i}+E^{(i)}\left(\frac{\alpha^{(i)}+h}{\sqrt{\beta}}\right)\right]
-orthogonal to
\psi_{\beta,n,\alpha_{i}^{h,+}}^{(i)}
.
Here, we made crucial use of the property (
100
) of the extended domain.
Furthermore,
\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u
is in the form domain of the self-adjoint operator
H_{\beta,\alpha^{h,+}_{i}}^{(i)}
, and thus the Courant–Fischer principle implies:

\left\langle H_{\beta,\alpha^{h,+}_{i}}^{(i)}\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u,\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u\right\rangle\geq\beta\lambda_{\mathfrak{N}_{i}(k-1)+1,\alpha^{h,+}_{i}}^{(i)}\|\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u\|^{2}\geq\beta\lambda_{k,\alpha^{h,+}}^{\mathrm{H}}\|\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u\|^{2},

(112)

where we used the identity (
81
).
The crude
L^{\infty}
bound (
89
) implies, since
\delta(\beta)^{-1}=\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}\left(\sqrt{\beta}\right)
, that
\left\|u\nabla\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}=\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\beta)\|u\|^{2}
.
At this point, we have shown that

\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}Q^{h}_{\beta}\left(\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u\right)-\left\|u\nabla\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\geq\beta\lambda_{k,\alpha^{h,+}}^{\mathrm{H}}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\|\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u\|^{2}+\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\beta)\|u\|^{2}.

We are left with the following term in (
110
):

\left\langle H_{\beta}^{h}\eta_{\beta}^{(N)}u,\eta_{\beta}^{(N)}u\right\rangle-\left\|u\nabla\eta_{\beta}^{(N)}\right\|^{2}.

Note that, since

\mathrm{supp}\,\eta_{\beta}^{(N)}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\bigcup_{i=0}^{N-1}B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right),

the fact that
V
is a smooth Morse function in
\Omega_{\beta}^{h}
ensures that there exist
C,\beta_{0}>0
such that, for all
\beta>\beta_{0}
,

|\nabla V|^{2}_{\big|\mathrm{supp}\,\eta_{\beta}^{(N)}}\geq\frac{1}{C}\delta(\beta)^{2},\qquad|\Delta V|_{\big|\mathrm{supp}\,\eta_{\beta}^{(N)}}\leq C,

hence:

\forall\,x\in\mathrm{supp}\,\eta_{\beta}^{(N)},\qquad U_{\beta}(x)=\frac{\beta^{2}}{4}|\nabla V(x)|^{2}-\frac{\beta}{2}\Delta V(x)\geq\frac{\beta^{2}\delta(\beta)^{2}}{4C}-\beta C.

Since
-\Delta\geq 0
, we have, for
\beta>\beta_{0}
and any
v\in H_{0}^{1}(\mathrm{supp}\,\eta_{\beta}^{(N)})
:

\left\langle H_{\beta}^{h}v,v\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathrm{supp}\,\eta_{\beta}^{(N)})}\geq\left\langle U_{\beta}v,v\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathrm{supp}\,\eta_{\beta}^{(N)})}\geq\beta\left(\frac{\beta\delta(\beta)^{2}}{4C}-C\right)\|v\|_{L^{2}(\mathrm{supp}\,\eta_{\beta}^{(N)})}^{2}.

Since
\sqrt{\beta}\delta(\beta)\xrightarrow{\beta\to\infty}+\infty
, we conclude that, for
\beta
large enough, and since
\eta_{\beta}^{(N)}u
belongs to
H_{0}^{1}(\mathrm{supp}\,\eta_{\beta}^{(N)})
, we have:

\left\langle H_{\beta}^{h}\eta_{\beta}^{(N)}u,\eta_{\beta}^{(N)}u\right\rangle\geq\beta\lambda_{k,\alpha^{h,+}}^{\mathrm{H}}\left\|\eta_{\beta}^{(N)}u\right\|^{2}.

Finally, we note that, owing to the condition (
86
), and the fact that the
\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}
have disjoint supports for
i=0,\dots,N-1
, it also holds:

\|u\nabla\eta_{\beta}^{(N)}\|^{2}=\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\beta)\|u\|^{2}.

In conclusion, we have shown that

\left\langle H_{\beta}^{h}u,u\right\rangle\geq\beta\lambda_{k,\alpha^{h,+}}^{\mathrm{H}}\sum_{i=0}^{N}\|\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}u\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})}^{2}+\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\beta)\|u\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})}^{2}.

(113)

The lower bound (
109
) follows easily since the
\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}
form a quadratic partition of unity on
\Omega_{\beta}^{h}
, that is

\forall\,v\in L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h}),\quad\|v\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})}^{2}=\sum_{i=0}^{N}\|\eta_{\beta}^{(i)}v\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\beta}^{h})}^{2}.

∎

5
Proof of Theorem
5

In this section, we derive finer asymptotics for the first Dirichlet eigenvalue
\lambda_{1,\beta}
of
-\mathcal{L}_{\beta}
, in the regime
\beta\to\infty
, as described by Theorem
5
.
The proof of (
39
), inspired by a construction performed in
[
41
]
in the case of a static domain, relies on the definition of accurate approximations of the first Dirichlet eigenvector for
-\mathcal{L}_{\beta}
, so-called quasimodes.
In Section
5.1
, we derive the necessary local estimates to ensure the well-posedness of the construction, in the presence of a temperature-dependent boundary. In Section
5.2
, we perform the construction of precise quasimodes, on slightly perturbed domains chosen so that the Dirichlet boundary condition is satisfied. In Section
5.3
, we present a technical result to deal with Laplace asymptotics in the presence of moving boundaries.
In Section
5.4
, we prove the key semiclassical estimates needed to conclude the proof of Theorem
5
, which is done in Section
5.5
.

5.1
Local energy estimates

A technical detail one has to address in this construction is that in order for the quasimodes (
128
) introduced below to be smooth and supported in
\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}
, we may need to reduce the value of
\delta(\beta)
in Assumption (
H2
) in order for various energy estimates to hold in the neighborhoods
B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))
of low-energy saddle points.
In fact, we show in Proposition
18
that there exists some constant
\varepsilon_{0}(V,z_{0})>0
independent of
\beta
such that requiring
\delta(\beta)<\varepsilon_{0}(V,z_{0})
(see (
EK4
)) allows for the construction of valid quasimodes.

We begin by recalling some facts concerning the geometry of the basin of attraction
\mathcal{A}(z_{0})
and the behavior of
V
near low-energy critical points.
Recall the definition (
31
).

Lemma 13
.

Let
z\in\partial\mathcal{A}(z_{0})
be a local minimum for
V|_{\mathcal{S}(z_{0})}
. Then,
\nabla V(z)=0
and
\mathrm{Ind}(z)=1
.

Proof.

From
[
50
, Theorem B.13]
, we may write
\mathcal{S}(z_{0})
as an at most countable disjoint union of submanifolds

\mathcal{S}({z_{0}})=\bigcup_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{Z}(z_{0})}\mathcal{W}^{+}(\mathbf{m}),

(114)

where
\mathcal{Z}(z_{0})
is the set of critical points of
V
on
\mathcal{S}(z_{0})=\partial\mathcal{A}(z_{0})\cap\bigcup_{z\in\mathcal{M}(V)\setminus\{z_{0}\}}\partial\mathcal{A}(z)
, which all have index at least
1
, and we recall the definition (
12
) of the stable manifold
\mathcal{W}^{+}
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{W}^{+}(\mathbf{m})
is a
(d-\mathrm{Ind}(\mathbf{m}))
-dimensional manifold.

From (
114
), there exists a unique critical point
\mathbf{m}
of
V
such that
z\in\mathcal{W}^{+}(\mathbf{m})
, so that by definition the gradient flow
\phi_{t}(z)
converges to
\mathbf{m}
in the limit
t\to\infty
, and is included in
\mathcal{S}({z_{0}})
, which is positively invariant for the gradient flow, as a union of stable manifolds. Since
V
decreases along trajectories of
\phi
and
z
is a local minimum of
V
on
\mathcal{S}(z_{0})
, it must hold that
\nabla V(z)=0
. In particular,
\mathbf{m}=z
.

It only remains to check that
\mathrm{Ind}(z)=1
. We first show that there exists
\mathbf{m}_{1}\in\mathcal{Z}(z_{0})
with
\mathrm{Ind}(\mathbf{m}_{1})=1
such that
z\in\overline{\mathcal{W}^{+}(\mathbf{m}_{1})}
. Assume for the sake of contradiction that this is not the case, hence there exists
r>0
such that

\mathcal{S}({z_{0}})\cap B(z,r)\subset\bigcup_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{Z}(z_{0})}}{{\mathrm{Ind}(\mathbf{m})\geq 2}}}\mathcal{W}^{+}(\mathbf{m}).

(115)

Since the set on the right-hand side has dimension at most
d-2
, the set
B(z,r)\setminus\mathcal{S}(z_{0})
is connected (see
[
33
, Theorem IV 4, Corollary 1]
). On the other hand, the sets

\mathcal{A}^{\prime}(y):=\overline{\mathcal{A}(y)}\setminus\mathcal{S}(y),\qquad y\in\mathcal{M}(V)

(116)

are open and pairwise disjoint, and the disjoint open cover

B(z,r)\setminus\mathcal{S}(z_{0})\subset\bigcup_{y\in\mathcal{M}(V)}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}(y)

(117)

has at least two non-empty components by definition of
\mathcal{S}(z_{0})
, and therefore cannot be connected.

We finally show that
\mathrm{Ind}(z)=1
. We already know that
\mathrm{Ind}(z)\geq 1
, and again assume for the sake of contradiction that
\mathrm{Ind}(z)>1
.
Since
\dim(\mathcal{W}^{-}(z))=\mathrm{Ind}(z)
and
\dim(\mathcal{W}^{+}(\mathbf{m}_{1}))=d-1
, there exists
v\in T_{z}\mathcal{W}^{+}(\mathbf{m}_{1})\cap T_{z}\mathcal{W}^{-}(z)
such that
v\neq 0
. Here,
T_{z}\mathcal{W}^{+}(\mathbf{m}_{1})
denotes the tangent half-space at the boundary, consisting of initial velocities of paths entering
\mathcal{W}^{+}(\mathbf{m}_{1})
starting from
z
.
Let then
f
be a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
map
[0,1)\to\{z\}\cup\mathcal{W}^{+}(\mathbf{m}_{1})\subset\mathcal{S}(z_{0})
such that
f(0)=z
and
f^{\prime}(0^{+})=v
.
Expanding, we get

V(f(t))=V(z)+\frac{t^{2}}{2}v^{\intercal}\nabla^{2}V(z)v+\mathcal{O}(t^{3}),

where we used
\nabla V(z)=0
twice. Since
V(f(t))\geq V(z)
for
t
sufficiently small, this implies
v^{\intercal}\nabla^{2}V(z)v\geq 0
. We have reached a contradiction, since
T_{z}\mathcal{W}^{-}(z)
is spanned by the negative eigendirections of
\nabla^{2}V(z)
.
∎

This leads to the following estimate on the position of
\partial\mathcal{A}(z_{0})
near a low-energy separating saddle point. Recall the definition of the local coordinates (
11
).

Lemma 14
.

For all
T>0
, there exist
\varepsilon_{0}(T),K(T)>0
such that for all
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}(T)
and all
i\in I_{\min}
,

\displaystyle\{y_{1}^{(i)}<-T\varepsilon\}\cap B(z_{i},K(T)\sqrt{\varepsilon})\subset\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})},

(118)

\displaystyle\{y_{1}^{(i)}>T\varepsilon\}\cap B(z_{i},K(T)\sqrt{\varepsilon})\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\mathcal{A}(z_{0}).

Proof.

Let
T>0
and
i\in I_{\min}
. In a neighborhood of
z_{i}
, the decomposition (
114
) shows that
\partial\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}=\mathcal{S}(z_{0})
coincides with
\mathcal{W}^{+}(z_{i})
. The stable manifold theorem (see
[
64
, Section 9.2]
) implies that
\mathcal{S}(z_{0})
is smooth in a neighborhood of
z_{i}
, with furthermore
\mathrm{n}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}}(z_{i})=v^{(i)}_{1}
.
Recall that
\sigma_{\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}}
denotes the signed distance function to
\partial\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}
with the convention (
5
). Its regularity in a neighborhood of
z_{i}
is guaranteed by the fact that, according to the definition (
34
),
z_{i}
is a separating saddle point.
Note that in the case where
z\in\partial\mathcal{A}(z_{0})
is a non-separating saddle point, the outward normal
\mathrm{n}_{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}(z)=-\nabla\sigma_{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}(z)
is not well-defined.
A Taylor expansion gives

\sigma_{\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}}(z_{i}+h)=\sigma_{\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}}(z_{i})-h^{\intercal}\mathrm{n}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}}(z_{i})+R(h)=-h^{\intercal}v^{(i)}_{1}+R(h)=-y_{1}^{(i)}(z_{i}+h)+R(h),

where
|R(h)|<M_{i}|h|^{2}
for some constant
M_{i}>0
and for all
|h|<M_{i}^{-1}
.
Choosing
\varepsilon_{0,i}=\frac{T}{M_{i}}
, we get, for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0,i}
and all
x\in\{y_{1}^{(i)}<-T\varepsilon\}\cap B(z_{i},K_{i}\sqrt{\varepsilon})
,

\sigma_{\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}}(x)\geq T\varepsilon-K_{i}^{2}M_{i}\varepsilon>0

(119)

for
K_{i}<\sqrt{T/M_{i}}
, thus
x\in\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}
for
K_{i}
sufficiently small.

Setting
\varepsilon_{0}(T)=\min_{i}\varepsilon_{0,i}
and
K(T)=\min_{i}K_{i}
, we obtain the first inclusion in (
118
).
The second inclusion follows along the same lines.
∎

The following proposition allows us to multiply
\delta(\beta)
by an arbitrarily small positive constant factor in Assumption (
EK3
), which will be convenient for the construction of accurate quasimodes.

Proposition 15
.

There exist
\varepsilon_{0}(V,z_{0}),C(V,z_{0}),\beta_{0}>0
such that, provided (
EK3
) holds and
\delta(\beta)<\varepsilon_{0}(V,z_{0})
, for all
0<\rho_{0}<1
, there exists
\rho<\rho_{0}
such that for all
\beta>\beta_{0}
, Assumption (
EK3
) again holds upon replacing
\delta(\beta)
by
\rho\delta(\beta)
and
C_{V}
by
C(V,z_{0})\rho^{2}
, i.e.

\left[\mathcal{A}(z_{0})\cap\{V<V^{\star}+C(V,z_{0})\rho^{2}\delta(\beta)^{2}\}\right]\setminus\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B(z_{i},\rho\delta(\beta))\subset\Omega_{\beta}.

(
EK3’)

Furthermore,
\varepsilon_{0}(V,z_{0})
can be chosen such that, for each
0\leq i<N
,
z_{i}
is the only critical point of
V
in
B\left(z_{i},2\varepsilon_{0}(V,z_{0})\right)
.

Proof.

We assume (
EK3
), and show (
EK3’
).
For any
C<C_{V}
, the inclusion
\{V<V^{\star}+C\rho^{2}\delta(\beta)^{2}\}\subset\{V<V^{\star}+C_{V}\delta(\beta)^{2}\}
implies that it is sufficient to show that, for each
i\in I_{\min}
, it holds, for some
C>0
and
0<\rho<1
, that

\mathscr{S}^{(i)}(\rho,C):=\mathcal{A}(z_{0})\cap\left\{V<V^{\star}+C\rho^{2}\delta(\beta)^{2}\right\}\cap\left[B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))\setminus B(z_{i},\rho\delta(\beta))\right]\subset\Omega_{\beta}.

(120)

The Taylor expansion of
V
in the coordinates (
11
) around
z_{i}
gives (recalling
V(z_{i})=V^{\star}
)

V-V^{\star}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\nu^{(i)}_{j}y^{(i)2}_{j}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{2}D^{3}V\left(y^{(i)-1}\left(ty^{(i)}\right)\right):y^{(i)\otimes 3}\,\mathrm{d}t=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\nu^{(i)}_{j}y^{(i)2}_{j}+\mathcal{O}(|y^{(i)}|^{3}).

(121)

It follows that the following estimate holds for all
i\in I_{\min}
and for some
M>0
on
B(z_{i},\frac{1}{M})
:

Q_{-}(y^{(i)})\leq V-V^{\star},

(122)

where we denote

Q_{-}(y)=M^{-1}|y^{\prime}|^{2}-My_{1}^{2}=M^{-1}|y|^{2}-(M+M^{-1})y_{1}^{2}.

(123)

We note that the set
\{Q_{-}>0\}
is simply given by the cone
\{|y_{1}|<M^{-1}|y^{\prime}|\}
.

We assume that
\delta(\beta)<M^{-1}
for sufficiently large
\beta
, and let
x\in\mathscr{S}^{(i)}(\rho,C)
.
It follows from (
122
) and
V(x)<V^{\star}+C\rho^{2}\delta(\beta)^{2}
that

Q_{-}(y^{(i)}(x))<C\rho^{2}\delta(\beta)^{2}
, from which we gather

\frac{M^{-1}|y^{(i)}(x)|^{2}-C\rho^{2}\delta(\beta)^{2}}{M+M^{-1}}<y_{1}^{(i)2}(x).

Therefore, for
0<C<M^{-1}
, and since
|y^{(i)}(x)|>\rho\delta(\beta)
, it holds that
|y_{1}^{(i)}(x)|>\sqrt{\frac{M^{-1}-C}{M+M^{-1}}}\rho\delta(\beta)
.
We take
C(V,z_{0})=C=\min(C_{V},M^{-1}/2)
, and set
\zeta:=\sqrt{\frac{M^{-1}-C}{M+M^{-1}}}>0
.
We distinguish two cases.

1.

If
y_{1}^{(i)}(x)<-\zeta\rho\delta(\beta)<\alpha^{(i)}/\sqrt{\beta}-\gamma(\beta)
, which holds for
\beta
sufficiently large, it holds asymptotically that
x\in\Omega_{\beta}
by (
H2
). This fixes
\beta_{0}
.

2.

On the other hand, if
y_{1}^{(i)}(x)>\zeta\rho\delta(\beta)
and
\delta(\beta)<K(\zeta)\sqrt{\varepsilon_{0}(\zeta)}
, applying Lemma
14
with
T=\zeta
and
\varepsilon=\delta(\beta)^{2}/K(T)^{2}
, it holds that
x\not\in\mathcal{A}(z_{0})
provided
\rho>\delta(\beta)/K(\zeta)^{2}
. This in turn implies
x\not\in\mathscr{S}^{(i)}(\rho,C)
, which contradicts the assumption on
x
and precludes this case.

It follows that, provided
\delta(\beta)<\varepsilon_{0}(V,z_{0}):=\min\{M^{-1},K(\zeta)^{2}/2,K(\zeta)\sqrt{\varepsilon_{0}(\zeta)}\}
, there exist
0<\rho\leq 1/2
and
C>0
, such that for sufficiently large
\beta
, the required inclusion (
120
) holds.
The conclusion of Proposition
15
follows by iterating this argument a finite number of times.

The final condition on
\varepsilon_{0}(V,z_{0})
can clearly be satisfied, since
V
has finitely many isolated critical points in
\mathcal{K}
.
∎

Remark 9
.

The constant
\varepsilon_{0}(V,z_{0})
is actually a function of
\min_{i\in I_{\min}}\min_{1\leq j\leq d}|\nu^{(i)}_{j}|
, where we recall the definition (
7
).

We finally state the following simple estimates.

Lemma 16
.

There exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0
,
0<C_{\xi}<1
and
0<M(V,z_{0})
such that, for all
i\in I_{\min}
and all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
, it holds that

\left[B(z_{i},\varepsilon)\setminus B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\right)\right]\cap\left\{|y_{1}^{(i)}|<C_{\xi}\varepsilon\right\}\subset\left\{V>V^{\star}+M(V,z_{0})\varepsilon^{2}\right\},

(124)

\mathcal{S}(z_{0})\setminus\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B(z_{i},\varepsilon)\subset\left\{V>V^{\star}+M(V,z_{0})\varepsilon^{2}\right\}.

(125)

Proof.

We recall the bound (
122
).
We first find
C_{\xi}>0
so that for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}:=M^{-1}
, (
124
) holds.
For any
0<C_{\xi}<1
and all
x\in\{|y_{1}^{(i)}|<C_{\xi}\varepsilon\}\cap\left[B(z_{i},\varepsilon)\setminus B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\right)\right]
, it holds that

Q_{-}(y^{(i)}(x))\geq\left[\frac{1}{4M}-C_{\xi}^{2}\left(M+\frac{1}{M}\right)\right]\varepsilon^{2}.

In particular, choosing
C_{\xi}<\frac{1}{2\sqrt{1+M^{2}}}
ensures that
V(x)-V^{\star}>Q_{-}(y^{(i)}(x))>0
, and taking
M(V,z_{0})<\frac{1}{4M}-C_{\xi}^{2}\left(M+\frac{1}{M}\right)
ensures (
124
).

Let us next show (
125
). Since
V
is decreasing along trajectories of the gradient flow
\phi_{t}
, it follows that
V|_{\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}}
is bounded from below by
V(z_{0})
. From the boundedness of
\mathcal{A}(z_{0})\cap\{V<V^{\star}\}
(see Assumption (
EK2
)) and the Morse property of
V
, the set
I_{\min}
is finite, and by Lemma
13
above, the points
(z_{i})_{i\in I_{\min}}
are the unique minimizers of
V|_{\mathcal{S}(z_{0})}
. Since
I_{\min}
is finite, the stable manifold theorem implies that the restricted Hessian
\nabla^{2}V|_{\mathcal{S}(z_{0})}
is positive definite in a neighborhood of
\{z_{i}\}_{i\in I_{\min}}
.
Therefore, there exist constants
C^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}>0
with
\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
such that for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
, it holds that

\mathcal{S}(z_{0})\setminus\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B(z_{i},\varepsilon)\subset\left\{V>V^{\star}+C^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\right\},

and choosing
M(V,z_{0})<C^{\prime}
suffices to ensure (
125
).
∎

5.2
Construction of the quasimodes on perturbed domains

From now on, we assume that assumptions (
EK1
), (
EK2
), (
EK3
) and (
EK4
) hold.

In the literature dealing with semiclassical asymptotics in the presence of a Dirichlet boundary (see e.g.
[
27
,
41
]
), a common way to define quasimodes is to express them in a dedicated set of local coordinates around each (generalized) critical point of interest, which is adapted to the local quadratic behavior of
V
and in which the geometry of the boundary becomes locally linear. This allows one to perform the analysis in a simplified geometric setting.
However, the flexibility regarding the specific geometry of the boundary afforded by Assumption (
H2
) makes the definition of such a set of local coordinates rather difficult. Instead, we base our argument on the following comparison principle for Dirichlet eigenvalues, or so-called domain monotonicity, which is well-known in the case of the Laplacian
V=0
.

Proposition 17
.

Let
A\subset B
be bounded open subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\beta>0
and
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.
Then

\lambda_{k,\beta}(B)\leq\lambda_{k,\beta}(A),

where we recall that
\lambda_{k,\beta}(A)
is the
k
-th eigenvalue of
-\mathcal{L}_{\beta}
with Dirichlet boundary conditions on
\partial A
.

Proof.

The fact that
X\in\{A,B\}
is bounded and open ensures that the Dirichlet realization of
-\mathcal{L}_{\beta}
on
X
has pure point spectrum tending to
+\infty
, due to the compact embeddings
H_{0}^{1}(X)\hookrightarrow L^{2}(X)
, and
\lambda_{k,\beta}(X)
is therefore well-defined.
The inequality follows immediately from the Min-Max Courant–Fischer principle and the inclusion of form domains:

H_{0,\beta}^{1}(A)\subset H_{0,\beta}^{1}(B).

∎

Our approach relies on the construction, for sufficiently large
\beta
, of two modified domains
\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}
such that

\Omega_{\beta}^{-}\subseteq\Omega_{\beta}\subseteq\Omega_{\beta}^{+},

(126)

and whose boundaries are flat in the neighborhood of low-lying separating saddle points close to the boundary. These domains are defined using Proposition
12
with
\rho(\beta)=2\gamma(\beta)
, and their boundaries are shaped like hyperplanes in the neighborhood of each
z_{i}
such that
\alpha^{(i)}<+\infty
, as made precise by Equation (
100
).

Remark 10
.

Since (
EK3
) holds for
\Omega_{\beta}
, it also holds for
\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}
. For
\Omega_{\beta}^{+}
, this is immediate since
\Omega_{\beta}\subset\Omega_{\beta}^{+}
. For
\Omega_{\beta}^{-}
, the proof of Proposition
12
shows that one can take
\Omega_{\beta}^{-}
to be a
\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\delta(\beta)^{2})
-perturbation of
\Omega_{\beta}
outside of
\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B\left(z_{i},\delta(\beta)\right)
. Since
V
is uniformly Lipschitz on
\mathcal{K}
, this implies that (
EK3
) still holds, possibly with a smaller constant
C_{V}
.

For similar reasons, choosing
\Omega_{\beta}^{-}
to be an
\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\delta(\beta))
-perturbation of
\Omega_{\beta}
near critical points
z_{i}
such that
\alpha^{(i)}=+\infty
, we may assume that
B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))\subset\Omega_{\beta}^{-}
for
\beta
sufficiently large, after possibly reducing
\delta(\beta)
by a constant factor.
We will henceforth assume that both these properties hold for
\Omega_{\beta}^{-}
.

Upon replacing
\delta(\beta)
by
c\delta(\beta)
for some
c\leq\frac{1}{2}
in Assumption (
H2
) (which is allowed according to (
EK4
) and Proposition
15
), we henceforth assume that, for
\beta
sufficiently large,

\forall\,i\in I_{\min},\qquad\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}\cap B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))=\left[z_{i}+E^{(i)}\left(\frac{\alpha^{(i)}}{\sqrt{\beta}}\pm 2\gamma(\beta)\right)\right]\cap B(z_{i},\delta(\beta)),

(127)

which will somewhat simplify the presentation.

The proof of Theorem
5
relies on the construction, inspired by
[
15
,
40
,
41
]
, of approximate eigenmodes
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}
for the Dirichlet realization of
-\mathcal{L}_{\beta}
on each of the domains
\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}
, which will be sufficiently precise to provide an asymptotic equivalent for
\lambda_{1,\beta}\left(\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}\right)
. The comparison principle of Proposition
17
will finally yield the conclusion of Theorem
5
.
More precisely, solutions to an elliptic PDE, obtained by linearizing
\mathcal{L}_{\beta}
in the neighborhood of each of the
\{z_{i},\,i\in I_{\min}\}
, are used to define these quasimodes locally, which are then rather crudely extended to define elements of the operator domains
H_{0,\beta}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm})\cap H_{\beta}^{2}(\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm})
.
Using Theorem
4
to ensure that a spectral gap exists, we can then use a resolvent estimate (Lemma
21
below) to control the error incurred by projecting the quasimodes onto the subspace spanned by the first eigenmode of the Dirichlet realization of
-\mathcal{L}_{\beta}
on
\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}
. This allows us to derive the Eyring–Kramers formula in a straightforward way.

Our quasimodes are defined by the following convex combinations

\psi^{\pm}_{\beta}=\frac{1}{Z^{\pm}_{\beta}}\left[\eta_{\beta}+\sum_{i\in I_{\min}}\chi_{\beta}^{(i)}\left(\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}-\eta_{\beta}\right)\right],

(128)

where the
\chi_{\beta}^{(i)}
are defined in (
87
), and where
Z^{\pm}_{\beta}
are normalizing constants imposing

\int_{\Omega^{\pm}_{\beta}}{\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}}^{2}\mathrm{e}^{-\beta V}=1.

This definition uses auxiliary functions
\eta_{\beta}
and
\left(\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}\right)_{i\in I_{\min}}
.

The function
\eta_{\beta}
is a crude cutoff function used to localize the analysis in some small neighborhood of the energy basin separating the minimum
z_{0}
from the low-energy saddle points
\{z_{i},\,i\in I_{\min}\}
.
More precisely, we define the energy cutoff
\eta_{\beta}
by

\eta_{\beta}(x)=\eta\left(\frac{V(x)-V^{\star}}{C_{\eta}\delta(\beta)^{2}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}}(x),

(129)

where
\eta\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is a model cutoff function chosen such that

\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\frac{1}{2})}\leq\eta\leq\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,1)},

(130)

and
C_{\eta}>0
is a constant we make precise in Proposition
18
below.
This rough construction is, in the neighborhood of low-energy saddle points, replaced by a finer local approximation, which one can (roughly) view as the solution to a linearized Dirichlet problem.

The functions
\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}
are defined, for
i\in I_{\min}
, by

\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}(x)=\frac{\displaystyle\int_{y_{1}^{(i)}(x)}^{\frac{\alpha^{(i)}}{\sqrt{\beta}}\pm 2\gamma(\beta)}\mathrm{e}^{-\beta\frac{|\nu^{(i)}_{1}|}{2}t^{2}}\xi_{\beta}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t}{\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\frac{\alpha^{(i)}}{\sqrt{\beta}}\pm 2\gamma(\beta)}\mathrm{e}^{-\beta\frac{|\nu^{(i)}_{1}|}{2}t^{2}}\xi_{\beta}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t}

(131)

where
\xi_{\beta}
is again a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
cutoff function used to localize the support of
\nabla\varphi_{\beta}^{(i)}
.
It is convenient for the analysis to work with a
\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}
whose gradient is localized around
z_{i}
. We thus take
\xi_{\beta}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
to be an even, smooth cutoff function satisfying

\mathbbm{1}_{\left(-\frac{C_{\xi}}{2}\delta(\beta),\frac{C_{\xi}}{2}\delta(\beta)\right)}\leq\xi_{\beta}\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\left(-C_{\xi}\delta(\beta),C_{\xi}\delta(\beta)\right)},

(132)

where
C_{\xi}>0
is a constant whose value we make precise in Proposition
18
below.

Remark 11
.

The functions
\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}
have a simple probabilistic interpretation. Observe that, upon formally taking
\xi_{\beta}=\mathbbm{1}_{[-\delta,+\infty)}
for some small constant
\delta(\beta)=\delta>0
, and
\gamma(\beta)=0
in the definition (
131
) (in which case we denote
\varphi_{\beta}^{(i)}=\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}
), an easy computation gives

\left\{\begin{aligned} \left((x-z_{i})^{\intercal}\nabla^{2}V(z_{i})\nabla-\frac{1}{\beta}\Delta\right)\varphi_{\beta}^{(i)}&=0\text{ on }E^{(i)}\left(\frac{\alpha^{(i)}}{\sqrt{\beta}}\right),\\
\varphi_{\beta}^{(i)}&=0\text{ on }\partial E^{(i)}\left(\frac{\alpha^{(i)}}{\sqrt{\beta}}\right),\\
\varphi_{\beta}^{(i)}&=1\text{ on }\partial E^{(i)}\left(-\delta\right).\end{aligned}\right.

The solution
\varphi_{\beta}^{(i)}
can be expressed as the so-called committor function (or equilibrium potential, see
[
14
]
)

\varphi_{\beta}^{(i)}(x)=\mathbb{P}_{x}\left(\tau^{(i)}_{\partial E^{(i)}\left(-\delta\right)}<\tau^{(i)}_{\partial E^{(i)}\left(\frac{\alpha^{(i)}}{\sqrt{\beta}}\right)}\right),

where
\tau^{(i)}_{A}
denotes the hitting time of the set
A\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
for the linearized dynamics around
z_{i}
:

\mathrm{d}X_{t}^{\beta,(i)}=-\nabla^{2}V(z_{i})\left(X_{t}^{\beta,(i)}-z_{i}\right)\,\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}}\,\mathrm{d}W_{t}.

The functions
\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}
should therefore be understood as approximating (locally around
z_{i}
) the probability of reaching the interior of the energy well before exiting the domain.

Proposition 18
.

There exist a bounded open set
\mathcal{U}_{0}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and positive constants
\beta_{0},C_{\xi},C_{\eta}>0
, such that for all
\beta>\beta_{0}
,
the functions
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}
defined in (
128
) and
\eta_{\beta}
defined in (
129
) satisfy the following conditions:

1.

\mathrm{supp}\,\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\subset\overline{\mathcal{U}_{0}}\text{ and $z_{0}$ is the unique minimum of\penalty\ $V$ in\penalty\ $\overline{\mathcal{U}_{0}}$},

(133)

2.

\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right),

(134)

3.

\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\in H_{0,\beta}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm})\cap H_{\beta}^{2}(\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}),

(135)

4.

\forall\,i\in I_{\min},\quad\mathrm{supp}\,\eta_{\beta}\cap\left[B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))\setminus B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right)\right]\subset\{y_{1}^{(i)}<-C_{\xi}\delta(\beta)\},

(136)

5.

\mathrm{supp}\,\nabla\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\setminus\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B\left(z_{i},\delta(\beta)\right)\subset\left\{V\geq V^{\star}+\frac{C_{\eta}}{2}\delta(\beta)^{2}\right\}\cap\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})},

(137)

6.

\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\equiv\frac{1}{Z_{\beta}^{\pm}}\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}\text{ on }B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right),

(138)

7.

\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\equiv\frac{1}{Z_{\beta}^{\pm}}\text{ on }\left[\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}\setminus\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B\left(z_{i},\delta(\beta)\right)\right]\cap\left\{V<V^{\star}+\frac{C_{\eta}}{2}\delta(\beta)^{2}\right\}\cap\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}.

(139)

Proof.

We take
C_{\xi}
as in Lemma
16
, and
C_{\eta}<\min\{C_{V},C(V,z_{0}),M(V,z_{0})\}
, where
C_{V}
is given in (
EK3
),
C(V,z_{0})
is given in Proposition
15
and
M(V,z_{0})
is given in Lemma
16
.
In the following proof, we reduce the value of
\delta(\beta)
several times by invoking (
EK4
) and Proposition
15
.

The properties (
137
), (
138
) and (
139
) are immediate consequences of the definitions (
128
), (
129
), and are verified by construction regardless of the value of
\delta(\beta)
.

The property (
136
) follows from (
129
), (
118
) and (
124
), once one imposes
\delta(\beta)
to be smaller than
\varepsilon_{0}(C_{\xi})
obtained in Lemma
14
and than
\varepsilon_{0}
from Lemma
16
, at least for
\beta
sufficiently large.

Let us now define
\mathcal{U}_{0}
and prove (
133
). For any local minimum
m\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
of
V
, standard arguments (see
[
64
, Chapter 8]
) show that the basin
\mathcal{A}(m)
is open and its boundary contains no local minimum of
V
.
Furthermore, for
\varepsilon<2\varepsilon_{0}(V,z_{0})
,
\overline{B(z_{i},\varepsilon)}
contains no local minimum of
V
for any
i\in I_{\min}
. Let us then define

\mathcal{U}_{0}=\left[\left(\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}\setminus\mathcal{S}(z_{0})\right)\cap\left\{V<V^{\star}+\varepsilon\right\}\right]\cup\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B(z_{i},\varepsilon).

Note that, according to (
H0
) and (
EK3
), there exist
\delta_{0},\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that

\mathcal{A}(z_{0})\cap\{V<V^{\star}+\delta_{0}\}\subset\mathcal{K}\cup\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B(z_{i},\varepsilon_{0}),

where the set on the right-hand side of the inclusion is bounded by (
EK2
) which implies that
I_{\min}
is finite. For
0<\varepsilon\leq\delta_{0}
,

\mathcal{A}(z_{0})\cap\{V<V^{\star}+\varepsilon\}\subset\mathcal{A}(z_{0})\cap\{V<V^{\star}+\delta_{0}\}

is bounded, so that
\mathcal{U}_{0}
is also bounded for a choice of
\varepsilon
sufficiently small. We may also choose
\varepsilon
sufficiently small so that
z_{0}
is the unique minimum of
V
in
\overline{\mathcal{U}}_{0}
.

Let us check that
\mathrm{supp}\,\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\subset\overline{\mathcal{U}_{0}}
.
For
i\in I_{\min}
,
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}
can be written as

\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\chi_{\beta}^{(i)}\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}\equiv\varphi_{\beta}^{(i)}\text{ on }B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right),\\
&(1-\chi_{\beta}^{(i)})\eta_{\beta}+\chi_{\beta}^{(i)}\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}\text{ on }B\left(z_{i},\delta(\beta)\right)\setminus B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right),\\
&\eta_{\beta}\text{ on }\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B\left(z_{i},\delta(\beta)\right),\end{aligned}\right.

using (
85
), (
88
) and the definition (
128
).

Therefore, it is clear that, for
C_{\eta}\delta(\beta)^{2},\delta(\beta)<\varepsilon
, it is the case that
\mathrm{supp}\,\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\subset\overline{\mathcal{U}_{0}}
:
the first condition ensures
\mathrm{supp}\,\eta_{\beta}\subset\overline{\mathcal{U}_{0}}
, while the second ensures
\mathrm{supp}\,\sum_{i\in I_{\min}}\chi_{\beta}^{(i)}\subset\overline{\mathcal{U}_{0}}
.
Reducing
\delta(\beta)
once again to satisfy these constraints, using (
EK4
) and Proposition
15
, (
133
) follows.

Let us show (
134
).
From (
133
),
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}
is compactly supported, and from the definition (
128
) and the smoothness, for each
i\in I_{\min}
, of
\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}\chi_{\beta}^{(i)}
on
B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))
, and since
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}
coincides with
\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}
on
B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right)
, it is sufficient to check that
\eta_{\beta}
is smooth on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right)
.
In turn, it is sufficient to show, from the definition (
129
), that
\mathcal{S}(z_{0})\cap\{V<V^{\star}+C_{\eta}\delta(\beta)^{2}\}
is contained in
\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right)
for
\beta
sufficiently large, where we use (
32
). This in turn follows immediately from the estimate (
125
), and from the choice
C_{\eta}<M(V,z_{0})
, provided
\delta(\beta)/2<\varepsilon_{0}
from Lemma
16
.

We finally show (
135
). From (
134
), it is clear that
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\in H^{2}_{\beta}(\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm})
. Thus it only remains to show
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\in H_{0,\beta}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm})
, for which we in fact show that
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}|_{\partial\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}}\equiv 0
holds. Again, we decompose the argument.
On
\partial\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}\setminus\left[\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}\cup\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))\right]
, it holds that
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\equiv\eta_{\beta}\equiv 0
, and on
\left[\partial\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}\setminus\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))\right]\cap\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}
, it holds that
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\equiv\eta_{\beta}\equiv 0
from the inclusion (
EK3
) and the choice
C_{\eta}<C_{V}
.
Now let
i\in I_{\min}
. If
\alpha^{(i)}=+\infty
, then
B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))\subset\Omega_{\beta}\subset\Omega_{\beta}^{+}
by (
H2
). By slightly reducing
\delta(\beta)
if necessary, it is possible to ensure that
B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))\subset\Omega_{\beta}^{-}
(see Remark
10
). Thus, in this case,
\partial\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}\cap B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))=\varnothing
.
We now assume
\alpha^{(i)}<+\infty
. From (
127
) and (
131
), the boundary condition
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}|_{\partial\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}}\equiv 0
is satisfied on
\partial\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}\cap B(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta))
, since on this set,
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\equiv\varphi_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}
.
On
\partial\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}\cap\left[B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))\setminus B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right)\right]
, this finally follows, for
\beta
sufficiently large, from the energy estimate (
124
), the choices
\delta(\beta)<\varepsilon
,
C_{\eta}<M(V,z_{0})
and the fact that, on this set
\left|y_{1}^{(i)}\right|=\left|\alpha^{(i)}/\sqrt{\beta}\pm 2\gamma(\beta)\right|\ll C_{\xi}\delta(\beta)
.
Since
\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}
are regular domains from Proposition
12
,
\psi_{\beta}^{\pm}\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm})=H_{0,\beta}^{1}(\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm})
by the trace theorem. The latter equality follows from the smoothness of
V
and the boundedness of
\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}
. This concludes the proof of (
135
).
∎

Figure 5:
Construction of the quasimode (
128
) in the neighborhood of the low-energy saddle point
z_{i}
, depicted in the adapted
y^{(i)}
coordinates. Here, we depict the elements entering into the construction of
\psi_{\beta}^{+}
, in the case
\alpha^{(i)}<+\infty
. The shaded blue cone corresponds to the positive superlevel set of the quadratic form

from the proof of Proposition

.

We assume, for the remainder of this work, and without loss of generality, that
\delta(\beta)
is asymptotically sufficiently small for the conclusions of Proposition
18
to hold.
We now derive the first preliminary result for the proof of the formula (
39
), which is a variant of the Laplace method for moving domains.

5.3
Laplace’s method on moving domains

We present in this section a key technical tool, a variant of the Laplace method for exponential integrals in the case where the domain of integration varies with the asymptotic parameter. Moreover, we allow for a minimum of the argument of the exponential lying outside of the domain, but close to the boundary in some scaling made precise in Proposition
19
.
We recall the symmetric difference of sets, which we denote by

A\triangle B:=(A\cup B)\setminus(A\cap B)=(A\setminus B)\cup(B\setminus A).

We show the following result.

Proposition 19
.

Consider a family
(A_{\lambda})_{\lambda>0}
of Borel sets. Assume that there exist
\mathcal{K},A_{\infty}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with non-empty interiors,
x_{0}\in\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{K}}
, and
\epsilon>0
such that the following properties hold:

1.

The set
\mathcal{K}
is compact, and the following inclusion holds:

\forall\,\lambda>0,\qquad A_{\lambda}\subseteq\mathcal{K}.

(
L1)

2.

The functions

f\in\mathcal{C}^{4}(\mathcal{K}),\qquad g\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathcal{K})

(
L2)

are such that

\underset{x\in\mathcal{K}}{\mathrm{Argmin}}f(x)=\{x_{0}\},\qquad\nabla^{2}f(x_{0})\geq\epsilon\mathrm{Id},\qquad g(x_{0})\neq 0.

(
L3)

3.

The domains admit a limit in the semiclassical scaling around
x_{0}
:

\mathbbm{1}_{\sqrt{\lambda}(A_{\lambda}-x_{0})}\xrightarrow[\lambda\to+\infty]{\text{a.e.}}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{\infty}}.

(
L4)

Then,

\int_{A_{\lambda}}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda f(x)}g(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\left(\frac{2\pi}{\lambda}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda f(x_{0})}\left(\det\nabla^{2}f(x_{0})\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}g(x_{0})\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{G}\in A_{\infty})\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon(\lambda))+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-\frac{r}{2}})\right)

(140)

as
\lambda\to+\infty
, where
\mathcal{G}
is a Gaussian random variable with distribution
\mathcal{N}\left(0,\nabla^{2}f(x_{0})^{-1}\right)
, and the dominant error terms are determined by:

\varepsilon(\lambda)=\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{G}\in\left[\sqrt{\lambda}\left(A_{\lambda}-x_{0}\right)\right]\triangle\,A_{\infty}\right),

and

\begin{cases}r=2,&\text{if }A_{\infty}=-A_{\infty},\\
r=1&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Remark 12
.

The conclusion still holds true assuming only that
\mathcal{K}
is closed but not necessarily bounded, requiring instead that
g\in L^{1}(\mathcal{K})
, and that there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that, for any
0<\delta<\delta_{0}
,

\gamma(\delta):=\underset{x\in\mathcal{K}\setminus B(x_{0},\delta)}{\inf}\,\{f(x)-f(x_{0})\}>0.

(141)

The proof of this variant is verbatim the same as the one given below, upon replacing
\gamma
by
\gamma(\delta)
with
\delta<\delta_{0}
in (
145
).

Proof of Proposition
19
.

Up to a translation by
x_{0}
and considering instead
\tilde{f}(x)=f(x)-f(x_{0})
and
\tilde{g}(x)=g(x)/g(x_{0})
, we may assume without loss of generality that
x_{0}=0
,
f(0)=0
and
g(0)=1
.

Let us denote by
\mathcal{Q}:=\nabla^{2}f(0)
the Hessian of
f
at the minimum, which, according to (
L3
), is bounded from below by
\epsilon>0
.
We make use of the following Taylor expansions, which are valid in view of (
L2
):

f(x)=\frac{1}{2}x^{\intercal}\mathcal{Q}x+R_{f}(x),\qquad R_{f}(x)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{2}D^{3}f(tx):x^{\otimes 3}\,\mathrm{d}t,

(142)

g(x)=1+x^{\intercal}\nabla g(0)+R_{g}(x),\qquad R_{g}(x)=\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)x^{\intercal}\nabla^{2}g(tx)x\,\mathrm{d}t.

(143)

Let
\delta>0
be such that
B(0,\delta)\subset\mathcal{K}
, which exists since
x_{0}\in\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{K}}
. We may furthermore assume (upon possibly reducing
\delta
), according to (
142
), that for some
C>0
,

\forall\,x\in B(0,\delta),\qquad f(x)\geq\frac{1}{C}|x|^{2},\qquad|R_{f}(x)|\leq C|x|^{3},\qquad|R_{g}(x)|\leq C|x|^{2}.

(144)

In addition, by (
L3
) and the compactness of
\mathcal{K}
:

\gamma:=\underset{x\in\mathcal{K}\setminus B(0,\delta)}{\min}\,f(x)>0.

(145)

Then, using the inclusion (
L1
):

\left|\int_{A_{\lambda}\setminus B(0,\delta)}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda f(x)}g(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda\gamma}\|g\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{K})}=\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda\gamma}),

(146)

since
g
is integrable on
\mathcal{K}
by (
L2
).

It remains to estimate

I(\lambda,1):=\int_{A_{\lambda}\cap B(0,\delta)}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda f(x)}g(x)\,\mathrm{d}x,

(147)

for which we introduce the following parametric integral, for
0\leq t\leq 1
:

I(\lambda,t):=\int_{A_{\lambda}\cap B(0,\delta)}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda\left(\frac{1}{2}x^{\intercal}\mathcal{Q}x+tR_{f}(x)\right)}g(x)\,\mathrm{d}x.

(148)

The role of
t
is to interpolate between the quadratic approximation of
f
around the minimum and
f
itself.

From (
144
) and (
L3
), we deduce that
c=\min\{\frac{1}{C},\epsilon/2\}>0
is such that

\forall\,x\in B(0,\delta),\,\forall\,0\leq t\leq 1,\qquad\frac{1}{2}x^{\intercal}\mathcal{Q}x+tR_{f}(x)=\frac{1-t}{2}x^{\intercal}\mathcal{Q}x+tf(x)\geq c|x|^{2}.

(149)

We then write, by Taylor’s theorem:

I(\lambda,1)=I(\lambda,0)+\frac{\partial I}{\partial t}(\lambda,0)+\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial^{2}I}{\partial t^{2}}(\lambda,t)(1-t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

(150)

with

\frac{\partial^{k}I}{\partial t^{k}}(\lambda,t)=(-\lambda)^{k}\int_{A_{\lambda}\cap B(0,\delta)}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda\left(\frac{1}{2}x^{\intercal}\mathcal{Q}x+tR_{f}(x)\right)}R_{f}(x)^{k}g(x)\,\mathrm{d}x.

(151)

We can then estimate, for
k=2
, in view of (
149
):

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial^{2}I}{\partial t^{2}}(\lambda,t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,\delta))}\lambda^{2}\int_{B(0,\delta)}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda c|x|^{2}}R_{f}(x)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

(152)

\displaystyle\leq K\lambda^{2}\int_{B(0,\delta)}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda c|x|^{2}}|x|^{6}\,\mathrm{d}x=\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1}),

uniformly in
t\in(0,1)
, where we used the change of variables
y=\sqrt{\lambda}x
to obtain the last equality.
It follows that

I(\lambda,1)=\left[I+\frac{\partial I}{\partial t}\right](\lambda,0)+\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1}).

(153)

The bracketed term may be rewritten as a Gaussian expectation:

\displaystyle\left[I+\frac{\partial I}{\partial t}\right](\lambda,0)

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{\lambda}\cap B(0,\delta)}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda\frac{1}{2}x^{\intercal}\mathcal{Q}x}\left(1-\lambda R_{f}(x)\right)g(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

(154)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{2\pi}{\lambda}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}|\det\mathcal{Q}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1-\lambda R_{f}({\mathcal{G}}/\sqrt{\lambda})\right)g({\mathcal{G}}/\sqrt{\lambda})\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\cap B(0,\sqrt{\lambda}\delta)}\right],

where
{\mathcal{G}}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathcal{Q}^{-1})
.
Before estimating the expectation, we write the following expansion allowed by (
L2
):

R_{f}(x)=\frac{1}{6}D^{3}f(0):x^{\otimes 3}+\widetilde{R}_{f}(x),\qquad\left|\widetilde{R}_{f}(x)\right|\leq\widetilde{C}|x|^{4}\,\text{on }B(0,\delta),

(155)

which, together with (
143
), gives almost surely:

\displaystyle\left(1-\lambda R_{f}({\mathcal{G}}/\sqrt{\lambda})\right)g({\mathcal{G}}/\sqrt{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{6}D^{3}f(0):{\mathcal{G}}^{\otimes 3}-\lambda\widetilde{R}_{f}({\mathcal{G}}/\sqrt{\lambda})\right)\left(1+\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{G}}^{\intercal}\nabla g(0)+R_{g}({\mathcal{G}}/\sqrt{\lambda})\right)

(156)

\displaystyle=1+\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left[{\mathcal{G}}^{\intercal}\nabla g(0)-\frac{1}{6}D^{3}f(0):{\mathcal{G}}^{\otimes 3}\right]+\lambda^{-1}S({\mathcal{G}},\lambda).

A straightforward computation and estimate using the bounds (
144
) and (
155
) shows that there exist
K,\lambda_{0}>0
such that, for all
\lambda>\lambda_{0}
, it holds, almost surely,

\left|S({\mathcal{G}},\lambda)\right|\leq K\left(1+|{\mathcal{G}}|^{6}\right).

(157)

In particular
\mathbb{E}[|S({\mathcal{G}},\lambda)|]=\mathcal{O}(1)
in the limit
\lambda\to\infty
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1-\lambda R_{f}({\mathcal{G}}/\sqrt{\lambda})\right)g({\mathcal{G}}/\sqrt{\lambda})\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\cap B(0,\sqrt{\lambda}\delta)}\right]

(158)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1+\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}P({\mathcal{G}})\right)\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in A_{\infty}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1+\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}P({\mathcal{G}})\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\cap B(0,\sqrt{\lambda}\delta)}-\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in A_{\infty}}\right)\right]+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1}),

where
P({\mathcal{G}})={\mathcal{G}}^{\intercal}\nabla g(0)-\frac{1}{6}D^{3}f(0):{\mathcal{G}}^{\otimes 3}
is an odd polynomial in
{\mathcal{G}}
. By symmetry, the first term in the sum is then given by:

\mathbb{E}\left[(1+\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}P({\mathcal{G}}))\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in A_{\infty}}\right]=\begin{cases}\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{G}}\in A_{\infty})&\text{if }A_{\infty}=-A_{\infty},\\
\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{G}}\in A_{\infty})\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}})\right)&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(159)

We are left with the task of estimating

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[(1+\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}P({\mathcal{G}}))\left(\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\cap B(0,\sqrt{\lambda}\delta)}-\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in A_{\infty}}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|1+\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}P({\mathcal{G}})\right|\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\setminus B(0,\sqrt{\lambda}\delta)}\right]

(160)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|1+\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}P({\mathcal{G}})\right|\left|\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}}-\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in A_{\infty}}\right|\right].

By a standard Gaussian decay estimate and the second condition in (
L3
), the term

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|1+\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}P({\mathcal{G}})\right|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{G}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\setminus B(0,\sqrt{\lambda}\delta)}\right]=\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\lambda}{3}\epsilon\delta^{2}})

is negligible with respect to
\lambda^{-1}
. Noting that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{G}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\triangle A_{\infty}}=\absolutevalue{\1_{\mathcal G\in\sqrt\lambda A_\lambda}-\1_{\mathcal G\in A_\infty}}
, we further obtain by a triangle inequality

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|1+\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}P({\mathcal{G}})\right|\left|\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}}-\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{G}}\in A_{\infty}}\right|\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{G}}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\triangle A_{\infty}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}|P({\mathcal{G}})|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{G}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\triangle A_{\infty}}\right].

The term
\varepsilon(\lambda)=\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{G}}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\triangle A_{\infty}\right)=\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(1)
by Assumption (
L4
). Let us show that
\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}|P({\mathcal{G}})|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{G}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\triangle A_{\infty}}\right]=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon(\lambda)+\lambda^{-1})
.
Decomposing this term, we obtain

\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}\left[\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}|P({\mathcal{G}})|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{G}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\triangle A_{\infty}}\mathbbm{1}_{|P({\mathcal{G}})|\leq\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right]&+\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}|P({\mathcal{G}})|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{G}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\triangle A_{\infty}}\mathbbm{1}_{|P({\mathcal{G}})|>\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right]\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{G}}\in\sqrt{\lambda}A_{\lambda}\triangle A_{\infty}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}|P({\mathcal{G}})|\mathbbm{1}_{|P({\mathcal{G}})|>\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right]\end{aligned}.

(161)

Furthermore, since
P(x)
is bounded by
C_{d,f}|x|^{3}\penalty\
for some constant
C_{d,f}>0
outside of a compact set, it holds, for sufficiently large
\lambda
, that

\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}|P({\mathcal{G}})|\mathbbm{1}_{|P({\mathcal{G}})|>\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right]\leq C_{d,f}\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}|{\mathcal{G}}|^{3}\mathbbm{1}_{|{\mathcal{G}}|^{3}>C_{d,f}^{-1}\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right]=\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-\epsilon\lambda^{1/3}/3})=\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1}),

(162)

by a Gaussian decay estimate.

In view of (
153
), and collecting the estimates (
152
), (
159
), (
160
), (
161
) and (
162
), we conclude that

I(\lambda,1)=\left(\frac{2\pi}{\lambda}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\left(\det\mathcal{Q}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{G}}\in A_{\infty})\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1})+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon(\lambda))+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}})\mathbbm{1}_{A_{\infty}\neq-A_{\infty}}\right),

(163)

which gives the claimed asymptotic behavior (
140
).
∎

Note that the same proof can be adapted to compute higher-order terms in the asymptotic expansion.

5.4
Low-temperature estimates

We obtain in this section the key estimates on the quasimode (
128
) needed to compute the asymptotic behavior of
\left\|\nabla\psi_{\beta}\right\|_{L_{\beta}^{2}(\Omega_{\beta})}
and
\left\|\mathcal{L}_{\beta}\psi_{\beta}\right\|_{L_{\beta}^{2}(\Omega_{\beta})}
in the limit
\beta\to\infty
.
Good estimates for these quantities, summarized in Proposition
20
, together with a resolvent estimate given by Lemma
21
below, will yield the modified Eyring–Kramers formula (
39
) in the proof of Theorem
5
, concluded in Section
5.5
.

For convenience, we decompose the analysis according to the following partition of the domain
\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}
:

\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}=\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}\left[\mathbf{A}_{\beta}^{(i)}\cup\mathbf{B}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}\cup\mathbf{C}_{\beta}^{(i)}\cup\mathbf{D}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}\right]\cup\mathbf{E}_{\beta}\cup\mathbf{F}_{\beta}\cup\mathbf{G}_{\beta}^{\pm},

(164)

where, for
i\in I_{\min}
:

1.

\mathbf{A}_{\beta}^{(i)}=\left[B\left(z_{i},\delta(\beta)\right)\setminus B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right)\right]\cap\left\{y_{1}^{(i)}<-C_{\xi}\delta(\beta)\right\}\cap\left\{V-V^{\star}\geq C_{\eta}\delta(\beta)^{2}\right\},

(165)

2.

\mathbf{B}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}=\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}\cap\left[B\left(z_{i},\delta(\beta)\right)\setminus B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right)\right]\cap\left\{|y_{1}^{(i)}|\leq C_{\xi}\delta(\beta)\right\},

(166)

3.

\mathbf{C}_{\beta}^{(i)}=\left[B\left(z_{i},\delta(\beta)\right)\setminus B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right)\right]\cap\left\{\frac{1}{2}C_{\eta}\delta(\beta)^{2}<V-V^{\star}<C_{\eta}\delta(\beta)^{2}\right\}\cap\left\{y_{1}^{(i)}<-C_{\xi}\delta(\beta)\right\},

(167)

4.

\mathbf{D}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}=\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}\cap B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right),

(168)

5.

\mathbf{E}_{\beta}=\left[\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}\cap\left\{\frac{C_{\eta}}{2}\delta(\beta)^{2}\leq V-V^{\star}\leq C_{\eta}\delta(\beta)^{2}\right\}\right]\setminus\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B\left(z_{i},\delta(\beta)\right),

(169)

6.

\mathbf{F}_{\beta}=\left[\overline{\mathcal{A}(z_{0})}\cap\left\{V-V^{\star}<\frac{C_{\eta}}{2}\delta(\beta)^{2}\right\}\right]\setminus\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right),

(170)

7.

\mathbf{G}_{\beta}^{\pm}=\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{i\in I_{\min}}\left[\mathbf{A}_{\beta}^{(i)}\cup\mathbf{B}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}\cup\mathbf{C}_{\beta}^{(i)}\cup\mathbf{D}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}\right]\cup\mathbf{E}_{\beta}\cup\mathbf{F}_{\beta}\right).

(171)

Note that, according to (
127
), the sets
\mathbf{B}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}
and
\mathbf{D}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}
have simple representations, namely

\mathbf{D}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}=\left\{y_{1}^{(i)}<\alpha^{(i)}/\sqrt{\beta}\pm 2\gamma(\beta)\right\}\cap B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right),

\mathbf{B}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}=\left\{y_{1}^{(i)}<\alpha^{(i)}/\sqrt{\beta}\pm 2\gamma(\beta)\right\}\cap\left[B\left(z_{i},\delta(\beta)\right)\setminus B\left(z_{i},\frac{1}{2}\delta(\beta)\right)\right]\cap\left\{|y_{1}^{(i)}|\leq C_{\xi}\delta(\beta)\right\}.

We refer the reader to Figure
6
for a pictorial representation of these sets.

\mathbf{D}_{\beta}^{(i),-}
\mathbf{B}_{\beta}^{(i),-}
\mathbf{B}_{\beta}^{(i),-}
\mathbf{A}_{\beta}^{(i)}

\{V=V^{\star}+C_{\eta}\delta(\beta)^{2}\}
\{V=V^{\star}+\frac{C_{\eta}}{2}\delta(\beta)^{2}\}
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\Omega_{\beta}^{+}
\partial\Omega_{\beta}^{-}
z_{i}

(a)

The case
\alpha^{(i)}<+\infty
. The boundaries
\partial\Omega_{\beta}^{\pm}
are hyperplanes inside
B(z_{i},\delta(\beta))
, separated by a distance
4\gamma(\beta)
, as described in (
127
).

\mathbf{D}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}
\mathbf{B}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}
\mathbf{B}_{\beta}^{(i),\pm}
\mathbf{A}_{\beta}^{(i)}
\mathbf{A}_{\beta}^{(i)}

\mathbf{E}_{\beta}
\mathbf{E}_{\beta}
